 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	//! Timing-critical maintenance and bulk catch-up share bounded engine slices.
     2	use super::{Shared, lock, refusal_json, respond};
     3	use serde_json::json;
     4	use snapback4_core::RuntimeError;
     5	use std::{sync::Arc, time::{Duration, Instant}};
     6	use tiny_http::Request;
     7	
     8	#[derive(Default, serde::Serialize)]
     9	struct Work { delivery_chunks: usize, expired: u64, schedules: usize, chunks: usize, parked: usize, #[serde(skip)] fired: Vec<String> }
    10	
    11	const WORK_TIME: Duration = Duration::from_millis(2);
    12	const CONTENDED_TIME: Duration = Duration::from_micros(250);
    13	
    14	// Charge consumed CPU for pacing, not time the OS already kept this thread off
    15	// CPU. The cooperative chunk deadline still uses wall time. A failed clock
    16	// sample falls back to the more conservative wall-time pause.
    17	struct SliceClock { wall: Instant, cpu: Option<Duration> }
    18	impl SliceClock {
    19	    fn start() -> Self { Self { wall: Instant::now(), cpu: Self::cpu_time() } }
    20	    fn cpu_time() -> Option<Duration> {
    21	        let mut at = libc::timespec { tv_sec: 0, tv_nsec: 0 };
    22	        if unsafe { libc::clock_gettime(libc::CLOCK_THREAD_CPUTIME_ID, &mut at) } != 0 { return None; }
    23	        Some(Duration::new(at.tv_sec.try_into().ok()?, at.tv_nsec.try_into().ok()?))
    24	    }
    25	    fn consumed(&self) -> Duration {
    26	        self.cpu.zip(Self::cpu_time()).and_then(|(start, end)| end.checked_sub(start)).unwrap_or_else(|| self.wall.elapsed())
    27	    }
    28	}
    29	
    30	// Detached readers do not take engine tickets. Sample OS load as well as the
    31	// round-3 engine contention signal; cache the syscall for 100 ms. Load includes
    32	// other tenants and trails short bursts, so this is a pacing hint, not admission.
    33	fn host_contended() -> bool {
    34	    use std::sync::{Mutex, OnceLock};
    35	    static SAMPLE: Mutex<(Option<Instant>, bool)> = Mutex::new((None, false));
    36	    static CPUS: OnceLock<usize> = OnceLock::new();
    37	    let mut sample = lock(&SAMPLE);
    38	    if sample.0.is_none_or(|at| at.elapsed() >= Duration::from_millis(100)) {
    39	        let mut load = 0.0;
    40	        let cpus = *CPUS.get_or_init(|| std::thread::available_parallelism().map_or(1, |n| n.get()));
    41	        sample.1 = unsafe { libc::getloadavg(&mut load, 1) } == 1 && load >= cpus as f64;
    42	        sample.0 = Some(Instant::now());
    43	    }
    44	    sample.1
    45	}
    46	
    47	fn engine_work<T>(shared: &Shared, run: impl FnOnce(&mut snapback4_core::Engine, Duration) -> T) -> T {
    48	    let mut engine = shared.engine.background();
    49	    lock(&shared.readers).retry_missing(&engine);
    50	    let budget = if engine.contended() || host_contended() { CONTENDED_TIME } else { WORK_TIME };
    51	    let result = run(&mut engine, budget);
    52	    // Publish from this guard: a second ordinary admission would both delay
    53	    // notification and mistake maintenance for a newly arrived request.
    54	    shared.publish_engine(&engine);
    55	    result
    56	}
    57	
    58	fn phase<T: Default>(shared: &Shared, result: Result<T, RuntimeError>, drain: bool, name: &str) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
    59	    match result {
    60	        Err(error) if !drain => { super::adoption::background_failure(shared, name, &error); Ok(T::default()) }
    61	        other => other,
    62	    }
    63	}
    64	
    65	// @ref LLP 3000#7-decisions-during-execution — lifetimes are swept into the partition log; missed schedule boundaries fire once.
    66	fn work(shared: &Shared, drain: bool, tick: u64) -> Result<Work, RuntimeError> {
    67	    work_at(shared, drain, tick, None)
    68	}
    69	
    70	fn work_at(shared: &Shared, drain: bool, tick: u64, schedule_sample: Option<i64>) -> Result<Work, RuntimeError> {
    71	    let mut work = Work::default();
    72	    shared.ephemeral.sweep(shared.now())?;
    73	    work.delivery_chunks = delivery(shared, drain)?;
    74	    loop {
    75	        if phase(shared, engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| engine.run_view_chunk_for(shared.now(), budget)), drain, "view recount")?.is_none() {break;}
    76	        work.chunks += 1;
    77	        if !drain && work.chunks >= 500 {break;}
    78	        std::thread::yield_now();
    79	    }
    80	    loop {
    81	        let swept = phase(shared, engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| engine.sweep_expired_for(shared.now(), 2_000, budget)), drain, "the expiry sweep")?;
    82	        work.expired += swept;
    83	        if !drain || swept == 0 { break; }
    84	    }
    85	    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.2 — advancing a replay clock does not release its schedule hold.
    86	    if !shared.hold_schedules {
    87	        let fired = phase(shared, engine_work(shared, |engine, _| {
    88	            // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.12.2 — select advance windows before draining; admission deadlines still use the live advanced clock.
    89	            if let Some(sample) = schedule_sample {
    90	                let windows = engine.schema().schedules.iter().enumerate().map(|(index, schedule)| (index, schedule.boundary(sample))).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    91	                let mut fired = Vec::new();
    92	                for (index, boundary) in windows { fired.extend(engine.fire_schedule_boundary_at(shared.now(), index, boundary)?); }
    93	                Ok(fired)
    94	            } else { engine.fire_schedules(shared.now()) }
    95	        }), drain, "schedules")?;
    96	        work.schedules = fired.len();
    97	        for name in &fired { snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4: schedule {name} fired"); }
    98	        work.fired = fired;
    99	    }
   100	    while drain || work.chunks < 500 {
   101	        if crate::lifecycle::stopping() { break; }
   102	        let (run, logs) = engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| {
   103	            let run = engine.run_job_chunk_for(shared.now(), budget);
   104	            (run, engine.take_native_logs())
   105	        });
   106	        for line in logs { snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4: native {line}"); }
   107	        let Some(run) = phase(shared, run, drain, "job")? else { break; };
   108	        work.chunks += 1;
   109	        match run.state {
   110	            snapback4_core::JobState::Done => snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4: job {} done: {} chunk{}, {} row{} written", run.name, run.chunks, if run.chunks == 1 { "" } else { "s" }, run.rows_written, if run.rows_written == 1 { "" } else { "s" }),
   111	            snapback4_core::JobState::Parked => {
   112	                work.parked += 1;
   113	                if shared.personas { shared.refusals.job(&run); }
   114	                snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4: job {} parked after {} chunks: {}", run.name, run.chunks, run.error.as_deref().unwrap_or(""));
   115	            }
   116	            _ => {}
   117	        }
   118	        // FIFO engine admission hands waiting requests their turn before the
   119	        // next chunk; yield_now alone cannot make that promise for a mutex.
   120	    }
   121	    if drain || tick % 5 == 0 {
   122	        loop {
   123	            let actions = phase(shared, engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| engine.requeue_expired_actions_for(shared.now(), budget)), drain, "the action lease sweep")?;
   124	            let effects = phase(shared, engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| engine.requeue_expired_effects_for(shared.now(), budget)), drain, "the effect lease sweep")?;
   125	            if actions + effects > 0 { shared.durable_committed(&lock(&shared.engine)); }
   126	            if effects > 0 { snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4: {effects} effect lease{} expired; queued again", if effects == 1 { "" } else { "s" }); }
   127	            if !drain || actions + effects == 0 { break; }
   128	        }
   129	    }
   130	    if drain || tick % 60 == 0 {
   131	        phase(shared, (|| {
   132	            let mut sweep = engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| engine.prepare_asset_sweep_for(shared.now(), budget))?;
   133	            sweep.remove_files()?;
   134	            while !sweep.is_empty() { engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| engine.finish_asset_sweep_for(&mut sweep, budget))?; }
   135	            Ok(())
   136	        })(), drain, "the asset sweep")?;
   137	    }
   138	    Ok(work)
   139	}
   140	
   141	fn delivery(shared: &Shared, drain: bool) -> Result<usize, RuntimeError> {
   142	    let mut chunks = 0;
   143	    while drain || chunks < 500 {
   144	        if crate::lifecycle::stopping() { break; }
   145	        if phase(shared, engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| engine.run_timeline_chunk_for(shared.now(), budget)), drain, "timeline delivery")?.is_none() { break; }
   146	        chunks += 1;
   147	    }
   148	    Ok(chunks)
   149	}
   150	
   151	// @ref LLP 3000#contended-read-serving — ordinary-priority bulk work yields
   152	// outside every shared lock. Aged service protects finite backlogs from traffic.
   153	const BULK_AGE: Duration = Duration::from_secs(1);
   154	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
   155	enum BulkKind { Delivery, Recount, Job }
   156	pub(super) struct State {
   157	    next: BulkKind,
   158	    serviced: Instant,
   159	    resume_at: Instant,
   160	    empty_pause: Duration,
   161	    advancing: bool,
   162	    // Explicit mutation-boundary tests pause all background maintenance until drain.
   163	    paused: bool,
   164	    // Separate clock callers (and a dev reset) may wait here; maintenance never takes this lock.
   165	    pub(super) advance: Arc<std::sync::Mutex<()>>,
   166	}
   167	impl Default for State {
   168	    fn default() -> Self {
   169	        let now = Instant::now();
   170	        Self { next: BulkKind::Delivery, serviced: now, resume_at: now, empty_pause: Duration::ZERO,
   171	            advancing: false, paused: false, advance: Arc::new(std::sync::Mutex::new(())) }
   172	    }
   173	}
   174	impl State {
   175	    fn aged(&self, now: Instant) -> bool { now.saturating_duration_since(self.serviced) >= BULK_AGE }
   176	    fn eligible(&self, now: Instant, aged_only: bool) -> bool {
   177	        !self.advancing && !self.paused && if aged_only { self.aged(now) } else { now >= self.resume_at }
   178	    }
   179	    fn completed(&mut self, now: Instant, elapsed: Duration, contended: bool, worked: bool) {
   180	        self.next = match self.next { BulkKind::Delivery => BulkKind::Recount, BulkKind::Recount => BulkKind::Job, BulkKind::Job => BulkKind::Delivery };
   181	        self.serviced = now;
   182	        // Target 1/8 CPU duty under contention, charging consumed thread CPU,
   183	        // including atomic overruns. Cap at 100 ms; aged service is independent.
   184	        let cap = Duration::from_millis(100);
   185	        let charged = if contended { (elapsed * 7).min(cap) } else { Duration::ZERO };
   186	        self.empty_pause += charged;
   187	        // @ref LLP 3000#contended-read-serving — empty kinds keep their CPU
   188	        // charge, but repay it with the next useful slice or the empty cycle.
   189	        // Avoid two extra scheduler wakeups for every single delivery slice.
   190	        let pause = if worked || self.empty_pause >= cap { self.empty_pause.min(cap) } else { Duration::ZERO };
   191	        self.empty_pause -= pause;
   192	        // An aged slice can finish after bulk flushes empty-cycle debt. Append
   193	        // this charge without discarding repayment still owed in that ordering.
   194	        self.resume_at = self.resume_at.max(now) + pause;
   195	    }
   196	
   197	    fn empty_cycle(&mut self, now: Instant) -> Duration {
   198	        let pause = std::mem::take(&mut self.empty_pause);
   199	        // A timed worker may just have scheduled repayment. Append remaining
   200	        // debt rather than overlapping (and losing) either charge.
   201	        self.resume_at = self.resume_at.max(now) + pause;
   202	        self.resume_at.saturating_duration_since(now)
   203	    }
   204	}
   205	
   206	// Admission owns no maintenance gate. Once admitted, the engine serializes the
   207	// cursor's read -> chunk commit -> advance; the gate only protects small state
   208	// accesses. Ordinary work never waits for a bulk thread to enter the engine.
   209	fn bulk_slice(shared: &Shared, aged_only: bool) -> bool {
   210	    if !lock(&shared.maintenance).eligible(Instant::now(), aged_only) { return false; }

     6	const version = value => /^\d+\.\d+\.\d+(?:-[0-9A-Za-z.-]+)?$/.test(value ?? '') ? value : undefined;
     7	export function lockedVersion(text) {
     8	  try {
     9	    const lock = JSON.parse(text);
    10	    const found = version(lock.packages?.['node_modules/snapback4']?.version ?? lock.dependencies?.snapback4?.version);
    11	    if (found) return found;
    12	  } catch { /* Bun lockfiles are JSONC; pnpm is YAML. */ }
    13	  const lines = text.split('\n');
    14	  const found = new Set();
    15	  for (let i = 0; i < lines.length; i++) if (/^\s*['"]?snapback4['"]?:\s*$/.test(lines[i])) {
    16	    for (const line of lines.slice(i + 1, i + 5)) {
    17	      const resolved = /^\s*version:\s*(.+)$/.exec(line)?.[1]?.trim().replace(/^['"]|['"]$/g, '').split('(')[0];
    18	      if (version(resolved)) found.add(resolved);
    19	    }
    20	  }
    21	  for (const match of text.matchAll(/snapback4@([^\s"'/:()[\]{},]+)/g)) if (version(match[1])) found.add(match[1]);
    22	  return found.size === 1 ? [...found][0] : undefined;
    23	}
    24	export function installedVersion(root) {
    25	  try { const require = createRequire(join(root, 'package.json')); return version(JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(dirname(dirname(require.resolve('snapback4/bin-path'))), 'package.json'), 'utf8')).version); } catch { /* Older copied trees may have no installed compiler. */ }

    27	}

    30	    try { const found = version(JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(root, '.snapback4', name), 'utf8')).version); if (found) return found; } catch { /* absent or invalid */ }
    31	  }

    35	  }

    38	export function acknowledgeVersion(root, version) {
    39	  const directory = join(root, '.snapback4'); mkdirSync(directory, { recursive: true });
    40	  const path = join(directory, 'runtime-version.json'), temporary = `${path}.${process.pid}.tmp`;
    41	  writeFileSync(temporary, JSON.stringify({ version })); renameSync(temporary, path);
    42	}
    43	export function compareVersions(a, b) {
    44	  const parts = v => { const i = v.indexOf('-'); return i < 0 ? [v, undefined] : [v.slice(0, i), v.slice(i + 1)]; };
    45	  const [ac, ap] = parts(a), [bc, bp] = parts(b);
    46	  const av = ac.split('.').map(Number), bv = bc.split('.').map(Number);
    47	  for (let i = 0; i < 3; i++) if (av[i] !== bv[i]) return av[i] - bv[i];
    48	  if (ap === undefined || bp === undefined) return ap === bp ? 0 : ap === undefined ? 1 : -1;
    49	  const aa = ap.split('.'), ba = bp.split('.');
    50	  for (let i = 0; i < Math.max(aa.length, ba.length); i++) {
    51	    if (aa[i] === ba[i]) continue;
    52	    if (aa[i] === undefined || ba[i] === undefined) return aa[i] === undefined ? -1 : 1;
    53	    const an = /^\d+$/.test(aa[i]), bn = /^\d+$/.test(ba[i]);
    54	    if (an && bn) { const av = aa[i].replace(/^0+/, ''), bv = ba[i].replace(/^0+/, ''); if (av === bv) continue; return av.length - bv.length || (av < bv ? -1 : 1); }
    55	    if (an !== bn) return an ? -1 : 1;
    56	    return aa[i] < ba[i] ? -1 : 1;
    57	  }
    58	  return 0;
    59	}
    60	export function runtimeChanges(history, previous, installed) {
    61	  if (!version(previous) || !version(installed) || compareVersions(previous, installed) >= 0) return '';
    62	  let selected = false, out = '';
    63	  for (const line of history.split('\n')) {
    64	    if (line.startsWith('## ')) { const v = line.slice(3); selected = !!version(v) && compareVersions(v, previous) > 0 && compareVersions(v, installed) <= 0; }
    65	    if (selected && line.startsWith('## ')) out += (out ? '\n' : '') + line + '\n\n';
    66	    else if (selected && line.startsWith('- ')) out += line + '\n';
    67	  }
    68	  return out ? `runtime changes: ${previous} → ${installed}\n${out}History begins at 0.4.26; earlier changes are not recorded.\n` : '';
    69	}
    70	export function installedHistory(root) {
    71	  // The parent process resolved the old package already. Resolve the new
    72	  // symlink in a fresh process, just like upgrade's post-install check.
    73	  const script = `import {createRequire} from 'node:module';import {join,dirname} from 'node:path';const require=createRequire(join(process.argv[1],'package.json'));console.log(join(dirname(dirname(require.resolve('snapback4/bin-path'))),'CHANGELOG.md'));`;
    74	  const result = spawnSync(process.execPath, ['--input-type=module', '-e', script, root], { encoding: 'utf8' });
    75	  if (result.error || result.status !== 0) throw Error('installed release history unavailable');
    76	  return readFileSync(result.stdout.trim(), 'utf8');
    77	}

+export async function proveDX6(directory) {
+  const parent=resolve(directory),scratch=mkdtempSync(join(parent,"dx6-"));

+  const env={...process.env,CHROME:chromiumExecutable()};delete env.SNAPBACK4_BIN;delete env.PORT_LO;delete env.PORT_HI;

+  try {
+    const clock=join(scratch,"clock");mkdirSync(clock);

+    await run(["consumer.mjs"],clock);

+c.commit()

+const r=spawnSync(process.execPath,[${JSON.stringify(wrapper)},'sql','SELECT * FROM items','--release',${JSON.stringify(join(clock,'staging'))},'--json'],{encoding:'utf8',timeout:30000});

   173	/** @internal Shared by the Node proof kit and the native browser driver. */
   174	export function webWitnessObserverScript(): string {
   175	  return `(() => {
   176	    const register = (${registerWebWitness.toString()});
   177	    let selected;
   178	    Object.defineProperty(window, Symbol.for("snapback4.proof.app"), { configurable: false, writable: false, value: app => {
   179	      if (selected) return;

   182	      const bridge = window.__snapback4Witness;
   183	      Object.freeze(bridge);

   185	    }});

   189	/** Run an event handler with an explicit write claim that survives awaited uploads.
   190	 * @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000.000#11 — only explicit per-arm custody survives an async handler.
   191	 * Call synchronously from the clicked control. Use the supplied mutate inside
   192	 * the callback; it carries only that arm's token, never another handler's writes.
   193	 */
   194	export async function witnessForm<T>(app: App, event: Event | { nativeEvent: Event }, handler: (mutate: App["mutate"]) => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {
   195	  const native = "nativeEvent" in event ? event.nativeEvent : event;
   196	  const bridge = typeof window === "undefined" ? undefined : (window as unknown as { __snapback4Witness?: {
   197	    claimHandler(owner: App, event: Event): { mutate: App["mutate"]; done(): void } | undefined;
   198	  } }).__snapback4Witness;
   199	  const claim = bridge?.claimHandler(app, native);
   200	  try { return await handler(claim?.mutate ?? app.mutate.bind(app)); }
   201	  finally { claim?.done(); }

# Rules

**Snapback 2 optimizes the edit-to-verified loop — the agent's building an app on it, and ours building it.**

Speed is the constraint. Everything below exists to keep the edit-to-verified loop short
and to stop this repository accreting apparatus faster than anyone can remove it. These
rules bind `snapback2/` and the LLPs numbered 1000 and up; Snapback 1 is baselined, not
exempt, and its numbers may only fall.

Each rule is **[check]** (enforced by `caps`) or **[review]** (enforced by a human).

## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit

- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
  archiving one — which is just removing the link. Snapback 2 aims to leave this at 15;
  Snapback 1's links are archived at promotion. **[check]**
- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
  files are built, not committed; so are benchmark run records under `benchmarks/runs/`,
  which are evidence, not source. The baseline is Snapback 1's oversize count on the day
  the rule landed; it may not grow, and `snapback2/` contributes zero to it. **[check]**

## Blocking checks

`caps` · `ref-check` · `snapback2/scripts/test.sh` · `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs --check` · `check-inventories`

## Loop shape

- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
  schedule, attributed to the breaking commit, and files a bounded set of issues. **[review]**
- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**

## Time budgets

Tracked every run of `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs` (appends `snapback2/metrics/loop.jsonl`).
A regression is a P0 with a name on it.

| | budget |
|---|---|
| Incremental rebuild after a one-line edit | 15s |
| Test what you just changed | 30s |
| Blocking gate suite | 60s |
| Full build, warm cache | 5 min |
| **`snapback2 check` on a program (the agent's product latency)** | **1s** |
| **`snapback2 doctor` boot-to-ready** | **0.5s** |

## Scope

- **`NOT-DOING.md` is binding.** Moving something onto the doing-list means writing why
  and taking something off. **[review]**
- **Delete; don't deprecate.** No compat shims or migration paths before promotion. **[review]**
- **Nothing is compiled, transpiled, or generated at runtime that could be built ahead of
  time.** **[review]**
- **A spec needs an implementer and a date, or it isn't written.** **[review]**
- **Only implemented specs bind.** A document with no running code behind it is a
  proposal, and a proposal cannot block a PR or justify a check. **[review]**

## Agents

- **Agents remove apparatus freely and add none.** An agent PR that adds a check, script,
  registry, config file, or design doc needs explicit human approval saying so. **[review]**
- **2026-09-06 session exception:** the user delegated the search implementation
  decisions; one `snapback2-core/build.rs` may generate pinned Unicode tables at
  build time, alongside the existing package build. No new gate, registry,
  runtime generator, or Unicode dependency is authorized by this exception. **[review]**
- **Snapback 2 LLPs are numbered 1000 and up**; `llp-create` must be told. **[review]**
- **Snapback 4 LLPs are numbered 3000 and up** (`snapback4/`, LLP 3000); it adopts these budgets
  and loop shape as its own and keeps its scope in `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`. **[review]**

## Shared machines

- Warm, named lanes, one per stream of work. Never clone fresh; reuse the worktree.
- **Never `git stash`.** Use a temporary commit.
- Kill only PIDs you recorded at launch. Never by name or pattern.

## 2026-10-22 — DX6: wave-22 gate, staging-clock and frontend repairs

Wave-22 fixes #5/#6/#8 are implemented under the amendment in
LLP 3000.000.000.001 §11. Seeded accounts carry their actual user kind;
incomplete online session literals use bearer-only transport, while complete
and local sessions retain shape validation. Migration advice applies to actual
transitions, acknowledges piped delivery and scans partial sessions and numeric
advance uses. SQL inspection refuses future store formats before reading rows.

Staging advances preserve live credential lifetime transactionally, with
SDK timing reconciliation before business-clock sampling. Explicit expiry mode
retains expiry tests. No-drain pauses maintenance before mutation deadline
tests; drain resumes without moving time and a failed drain stays paused.
Blocked saved schedule boundaries refuse before effects with an executable
recovery duration. Clock reports coerce to now for numeric JavaScript callers.

Starter lists use ReadState and password clients omit witness boilerplate.
The proof driver owns the locked public observer channel (trusted same-origin
application scripts, no new auth boundary). Composed visible text matching
retains exactness and ambiguity, excluding hidden/editable text from signals.
Copied wave-22 frontend reductions are 336/354/354 non-whitespace characters.

and proposal hashes are under llp/reviews/dx6. The exact verification results,

    10	const MAX_TIME: i64 = snapback4_core::value::I53_MAX;
    11	
    12	pub fn input(message: impl Into<String>) -> Refusal { Refusal::new("E_CLOCK_INPUT", "clock", message).guide("clock") }
    13	pub fn release() -> Refusal { Refusal::new("E_CLOCK_DEV_ONLY", "clock", "clock controls require dev or a deploy --staging store; production always uses wall time").guide("clock") }

    15	/// Isolated time tests stay inside an assigned range, or request a free port.
    16	pub(crate) fn isolated_command() -> String {
    17	    isolated_range(std::env::var("PORT_LO").ok().as_deref(), std::env::var("PORT_HI").ok().as_deref())
    18	}
    19	fn isolated_range(lo: Option<&str>, hi: Option<&str>) -> String {
    20	    match (lo.and_then(|n| n.parse::<u16>().ok()), hi.and_then(|n| n.parse::<u16>().ok())) {
    21	        (Some(lo), Some(hi)) if lo > 0 && hi >= lo => format!("dev --memory --port {lo}-{hi} within the current range"),
    22	        _ => "dev --memory --port 0".into(),
    23	    }
    24	}
    25	
    26	pub fn duration(text: &str) -> Result<i64, Refusal> {
    27	    if text.starts_with('-') { return Err(input("clock advance cannot move backwards; use a positive duration such as +2m")); }
    28	    let text = text.strip_prefix('+').unwrap_or(text);
    29	    let split = text.bytes().position(|b| !b.is_ascii_digit()).unwrap_or(text.len());
    30	    let (number, unit) = text.split_at(split);
    31	    let multiplier = match unit { "ms" => 1, "s" => 1_000, "m" => 60_000, "h" => 3_600_000, "d" => 86_400_000, "w" => 604_800_000, _ => 0 };
    32	    number.parse::<i64>().ok().and_then(|n| n.checked_mul(multiplier)).filter(|n| *n > 0 && *n <= MAX_TIME)
    33	        .ok_or_else(|| input("expected a positive whole duration with ms, s, m, h, d or w, such as +25h"))
    34	}
    35	
    36	pub fn offset(text: &str) -> Result<i64, Refusal> {
    37	    if let Some(rest) = text.strip_prefix('-') { duration(rest).map(|n| -n) } else { duration(text) }
    38	}
    39	
    40	fn time(value: Option<i64>) -> Result<i64, Refusal> {
    41	    value.filter(|n| (0..=MAX_TIME).contains(n)).ok_or_else(|| input("time must be Unix milliseconds in 0..9007199254740991; choose a smaller time or duration"))
    42	}
    43	
    44	pub fn run_now(text: Option<&String>) -> Result<i64, Refusal> {
    45	    let now = snapback4_core::now_ms();
    46	    match text {
    47	        None => Ok(now),
    48	        Some(text) if text.starts_with('+') => time(now.checked_add(duration(text)?)),
    49	        Some(text) => time(text.parse().ok()),
    50	    }
    51	}
    52	
    53	#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
    54	pub struct Clock {
    55	    dev: bool,
    56	    base: i64,
    57	    offset: i64,
    58	    started: Instant,
    59	    epoch: String,
    60	    revision: u64,
    61	    #[cfg(test)]
    62	    frozen: bool,
    63	}
    64	
    65	impl Clock {
    66	    pub fn new(dev: bool, offset: i64) -> Result<Self, Refusal> {
    67	        if !dev && offset != 0 { return Err(release()); }
    68	        Ok(Self { dev, offset, base: time(snapback4_core::now_ms().checked_add(offset))?, started: Instant::now(),
    69	            epoch: snapback4_core::ids::mint(snapback4_core::now_ms(), &mut snapback4_core::ids::Mint::default()).map_err(|e| e.refusal())?, revision: 0, #[cfg(test)] frozen: false })
    70	    }
    71	    #[cfg(test)]
    72	    pub(crate) fn freeze(&mut self, now: i64) { self.base = now; self.frozen = true; }
    73	    pub fn now(&self) -> i64 {
    74	        #[cfg(test)] if self.frozen { return self.base; }
    75	        if !self.dev { return snapback4_core::now_ms(); }
    76	        self.base.saturating_add(self.started.elapsed().as_millis().min(MAX_TIME as u128) as i64).min(MAX_TIME)
    77	    }
    78	    /// Identifies samples taken before/after a restart or deliberate clock jump.
    79	    pub fn offset(&self) -> i64 { self.offset }
    80	    pub fn revision(&self) -> String { format!("{}:{}", self.epoch, self.revision) }
    81	    pub fn presence_now(&self) -> i64 {
    82	        // Production grace is elapsed real time, unaffected by wall-clock corrections.
    83	        if self.dev { self.now() } else { self.started.elapsed().as_millis().min(MAX_TIME as u128) as i64 }
    84	    }
    85	    /// Back to wall time: the offset `--clock-offset` and every advance added
    86	    /// is dropped (a development reset's `{"clock":"reset"}`).
    87	    pub fn reset(&mut self) -> Result<i64, Refusal> {
    88	        if !self.dev { return Err(release()); }
    89	        self.revision += 1;
    90	        self.offset = 0;
    91	        self.base = snapback4_core::now_ms();
    92	        self.started = Instant::now();
    93	        #[cfg(test)] { self.frozen = false; }
    94	        Ok(self.base)
    95	    }
    96	    pub fn advance(&mut self, milliseconds: i64) -> Result<i64, Refusal> {
    97	        if !self.dev { return Err(release()); }
    98	        if milliseconds <= 0 { return Err(input("clock advance cannot move backwards; use a positive duration such as +2m")); }
    99	        let next = time(self.now().checked_add(milliseconds))?;
   100	        let offset = self.offset.checked_add(milliseconds).ok_or_else(|| input("cumulative clock offset overflow"))?;
   101	        self.revision += 1;
   102	        self.offset = offset;
   103	        self.base = next;
   104	        self.started = Instant::now();
   105	        Ok(next)
   106	    }
   107	}

     1	//! Bounded, read-only evidence from the store's own commit clock.
     2	use snapback4_core::{Engine, RuntimeError};
     3	use std::collections::BTreeSet;
     4	
     5	const SKIPPED_PREVIEW: usize = 32;
     6	const TOLERANCE_MS: i64 = 5_000;
     7	const RECENT_COMMITS: usize = 64;
     8	const TABLE_BYTES: usize = 4096;
     9	const TABLE_NAMES: usize = 8;
    10	const REPORT_ERROR_BYTES: usize = 1024;
    11	// Reserve one MiB for the outer clock/work envelope inside the host encoder.
    12	// The reply retains `schedules` and its fired/held partition.
    13	const REPORT_BYTE_BUDGET: usize = (crate::serve::JSON_RESPONSE_CEILING - (1 << 20)) / 2;
    14	
    15	// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.12 — inspect a fixed recent commit window, never application row values.
    16	pub fn ahead(engine: &Engine, now: i64) -> Result<Option<String>, RuntimeError> {
    17	    // Reverse primary-key traversal: no full scan, sort, row payloads or retained
    18	    // historical stamp set. octet_length(column) uses SQLite record metadata;
    19	    // unlike a text length/cast/substr, it need not load oversized payloads.
    20	    let mut lead = 0;
    21	    let mut tables = BTreeSet::new();
    22	    let mut omitted = false;
    23	    engine.store().visit_recent_commit_clock(now.saturating_add(TOLERANCE_MS), TABLE_BYTES as i64, RECENT_COMMITS as i64, |record| {
    24	        let at = record.at;
    25	        if at <= now.saturating_add(TOLERANCE_MS) { return Ok(()); }
    26	        lead = lead.max(at.saturating_sub(now));
    27	        let length = record.length.ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Store("commit clock record is missing touched length".into()))?;
    28	        if length > TABLE_BYTES as i64 { omitted = true; return Ok(()); }
    29	        let bytes = record.touched.ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Store("commit clock record is missing touched bytes".into()))?;
    30	        match serde_json::from_slice::<Vec<String>>(&bytes) {
    31	            Ok(names) => for name in names {
    32	                if tables.contains(&name) { continue; }
    33	                if tables.len() < TABLE_NAMES && name.len() <= 128 { tables.insert(name); }
    34	                else { omitted = true; }
    35	            },
    36	            Err(_) => omitted = true,
    37	        }
    38	        Ok(())
    39	    })?;
    40	    if lead == 0 { return Ok(None); }
    41	    let mut names = tables.into_iter().collect::<Vec<_>>();
    42	    if omitted { names.push("additional tables omitted".into()); }
    43	    if names.is_empty() { names.push("no table names recorded".into()); }
    44	    Ok(Some(format!(
    45	        "N_CLOCK_STORE_AHEAD: recent commit stamps lead the current clock by up to {lead}ms (latest {RECENT_COMMITS} commits; tables: {}); use a fresh store per offset test ({}), or restart with the same --clock-offset (or dev --resume-clock) to continue",
    46	        names.join(", "), crate::dev_clock::isolated_command())))
    47	}
    48	
    49	/// UTC calendar text for Unix milliseconds: schedule boundaries are UTC.
    50	pub fn utc(ms: i64) -> String {
    51	    let (days, rem) = (ms.div_euclid(86_400_000), ms.rem_euclid(86_400_000));
    52	    // Civil-from-days (proleptic Gregorian), exact for the I53 range.
    53	    let z = days + 719_468;
    54	    let era = z.div_euclid(146_097);
    55	    let doe = z - era * 146_097;
    56	    let yoe = (doe - doe / 1_460 + doe / 36_524 - doe / 146_096) / 365;
    57	    let doy = doe - (365 * yoe + yoe / 4 - yoe / 100);
    58	    let mp = (5 * doy + 2) / 153;
    59	    let day = doy - (153 * mp + 2) / 5 + 1;
    60	    let month = if mp < 10 { mp + 3 } else { mp - 9 };
    61	    let year = yoe + era * 400 + i64::from(month <= 2);
    62	    format!("{year:04}-{month:02}-{day:02}T{:02}:{:02}:{:02}Z", rem / 3_600_000, rem % 3_600_000 / 60_000, rem % 60_000 / 1_000)
    63	}
    64	
    65	/// A positive duration in the units `clock advance` accepts, rounded up to a second.
    66	pub fn span(ms: i64) -> String {
    67	    let seconds = (ms.max(0) + 999) / 1_000;
    68	    let (d, h, m, s) = (seconds / 86_400, seconds % 86_400 / 3_600, seconds % 3_600 / 60, seconds % 60);
    69	    let parts: Vec<String> = [(d, "d"), (h, "h"), (m, "m"), (s, "s")].into_iter().filter(|(n, _)| *n > 0).map(|(n, unit)| format!("{n}{unit}")).collect();
    70	    if parts.is_empty() { "0s".into() } else { parts.join("") }
    71	}
    72	
    73	/// Each schedule's saved boundary, in declaration order.
    74	pub fn saved_boundaries(engine: &Engine) -> Vec<Option<i64>> {
    75	    engine.schema().schedules.iter()
    76	        .map(|schedule| {
    77	            let shape = engine.store().schedule_shape(&schedule.name).ok().flatten();
    78	            if shape.as_ref().is_some_and(|shape| *shape != snapback4_core::value::digest(schedule)) { return None; }
    79	            engine.store().schedule_boundary(&schedule.name).ok().flatten()
    80	        })
    81	        .collect()
    82	}
    83	
    84	/// Preflight is bounded before the clock or worker leases change.
    85	// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.12.1 — only explicit execution enumerates all crossed windows; coalesced reports keep a bounded preview.
    86	pub fn crossed_boundaries(engine: &Engine, before: &[Option<i64>], now: i64, held: bool, each_boundary: bool) -> Result<Vec<(usize, i64)>, snapback4_core::Refusal> {
    87	    let mut boundaries = Vec::new();
    88	    let mut report_bytes = 0usize;
    89	    for (index, schedule) in engine.schema().schedules.iter().enumerate() {
    90	        // JSON may escape every error byte; count that bound per schedule.
    91	        report_bytes = report_bytes.saturating_add(2048 + 6 * (REPORT_ERROR_BYTES + 3) + 8 * (schedule.name.len() + schedule.target.len()));
    92	        if report_bytes > REPORT_BYTE_BUDGET { return Err(crate::dev_clock::input("schedule report exceeds its byte budget; reduce the declared schedules")); }
    93	        if held { continue; }
    94	        let Some(old) = before.get(index).copied().flatten() else { continue; };
    95	        let end = schedule.boundary(now);
    96	        if old >= end { continue; }
    97	        let periods = schedule.periods_between(old, end);
    98	        if each_boundary && periods > (10_000 - boundaries.len()) as i64 {
    99	            return Err(crate::dev_clock::input("--each-boundary crosses more than 10000 schedule windows; use a smaller duration or omit --each-boundary to coalesce"));
   100	        }
   101	        report_bytes = report_bytes.saturating_add((if each_boundary { periods as usize } else { (periods as usize).min(SKIPPED_PREVIEW + 1) }).saturating_mul(1024 + 8 * schedule.target.len()));
   102	        if report_bytes > REPORT_BYTE_BUDGET { return Err(crate::dev_clock::input("schedule window report exceeds its byte budget; use a smaller duration")); }
   103	        if !each_boundary { continue; }
   104	        let mut boundary = schedule.next(old);
   105	        while boundary <= end {
   106	            boundaries.push((index, boundary));
   107	            boundary = schedule.next(boundary);
   108	        }
   109	    }
   110	    boundaries.sort_by_key(|(index, at)| (*at, *index));
   111	    Ok(boundaries)
   112	}
   113	
   114	// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#11-wave-22-loop-repairs-dx6 — no silent successful advance behind retained schedule markers.
   115	pub fn refuse_held(engine: &Engine, before: &[Option<i64>], now: i64) -> Result<(), snapback4_core::Refusal> {
   116	    for (schedule, saved) in engine.schema().schedules.iter().zip(before) {
   117	        if let Some(saved) = saved.filter(|saved| *saved >= schedule.boundary(now) && *saved > schedule.boundary(snapback4_core::now_ms())) {
   118	            let due = schedule.next(saved);
   119	            return Err(snapback4_core::Refusal::new("E_CLOCK_HELD", "clock", format!(
   120	                "schedule {} already processed boundary {}; requested clock {} has not reached its next window {}. Nothing advanced. Advance {} or use a fresh staging deployment; allowHeld:true explicitly accepts this hold. Never rewind saved boundaries",
   121	                schedule.name, utc(saved), utc(now), utc(due), format!("{}ms", due - now))).guide("clock"));
   122	        }
   123	    }
   124	    Ok(())
   125	}
   126	
   127	fn period(schedule: &snapback4_core::schema::Schedule) -> String {
   128	    schedule.describe()
   129	}
   130	
   131	/// What each schedule did on one clock advance, and why one did not fire.
   132	/// The core fires each schedule at most once per advance, for the latest
   133	/// boundary, and holds it while its saved boundary is ahead of the clock.
   134	// @ref LLP 3000#7-decisions-during-execution — missed boundaries fire once; this only explains them.
   135	pub fn schedule_report(engine: &Engine, before: &[Option<i64>], fired: &[String], now: i64, held: bool, each_boundary: bool, port: Option<u16>) -> Vec<serde_json::Value> {
   136	    let after = saved_boundaries(engine);
   137	    // Read only bounded summaries: job args/cursors and full error payloads
   138	    // are irrelevant to this report and must not be loaded then replicated.
   139	    let runs = job_summaries(engine).unwrap_or_default();
   140	    engine.schema().schedules.iter().enumerate().map(|(index, schedule)| {
   141	        let (old, saved) = (before.get(index).copied().flatten(), after.get(index).copied().flatten());
   142	        let current = schedule.boundary(now);
   143	        let next = schedule.next(current);
   144	        let mut entry = serde_json::json!({ "name": schedule.name, "target": schedule.target, "period": period(schedule), "fired": false, "firedWindows": [], "skippedBoundaries": [], "nextBoundary": utc(next) });
   145	        if fired.contains(&schedule.name) {
   146	            let end = saved.unwrap_or(current);
   147	            entry["fired"] = true.into();
   148	            let start = schedule.previous(end);
   149	            let window = |boundary| serde_json::json!({"periodStart": schedule.previous(boundary), "periodEnd": boundary});
   150	            let mut windows = Vec::new();
   151	            if each_boundary {
   152	                if let Some(old) = old {
   153	                    let mut at = schedule.next(old);
   154	                    while at <= end { windows.push(window(at)); at = schedule.next(at); }
   155	                }
   156	            } else { windows.push(window(end)); }
   157	            if each_boundary {
   158	                entry["reason"] = format!("fired {} windows: {}", windows.len(), windows.iter().map(|window| format!("{} to {}", utc(window["periodStart"].as_i64().unwrap()), utc(window["periodEnd"].as_i64().unwrap()))).collect::<Vec<_>>().join("; ")).into();
   159	            }
   160	            entry["firedWindows"] = windows.into();
   161	            entry["periodStart"] = start.into();
   162	            entry["periodEnd"] = end.into();
   163	            if !each_boundary { entry["reason"] = format!("fired for the period {} to {}", utc(start), utc(end)).into(); }
   164	            if let Some(run) = runs.get(&schedule.target) { entry["run"] = run.clone(); }
   165	            // @ref LLP 3000.040#24-monthly-and-yearly-schedules — calendar periods count boundaries, not milliseconds.
   166	            let skipped = if each_boundary { 0 } else { old.map(|old| schedule.periods_between(old, end) - 1).unwrap_or(0) };
   167	            if skipped > 0 {
   168	                entry["skippedPeriods"] = skipped.into();
   169	                let mut windows = Vec::new();
   170	                let mut at = schedule.next(old.unwrap());
   171	                while at < end && windows.len() < SKIPPED_PREVIEW {
   172	                    let mut item = window(at);
   173	                    let mut args = serde_json::Map::new();
   174	                    if let Some(program) = engine.program(&schedule.target) {
   175	                        if program.args.contains_key("periodStart") { args.insert("periodStart".into(), schedule.previous(at).into()); }
   176	                        if program.args.contains_key("periodEnd") { args.insert("periodEnd".into(), at.into()); }
   177	                    }
   178	                    item["command"] = format!("snapback4 run {} '{}'{}", schedule.target, serde_json::Value::Object(args), port.map(|port| format!(" --port {port}")).unwrap_or_default()).into();
   179	                    windows.push(item); at = schedule.next(at);
   180	                }
   181	                entry["skippedBoundariesOmitted"] = (skipped - windows.len() as i64).into();
   182	                entry["skippedBoundaries"] = windows.into();
   183	                entry["reason"] = format!("fired once, for the latest period {} to {}; {skipped} earlier period{} this advance crossed (from {}) did not run: missed boundaries fire once. Run {} with periodStart/periodEnd for an earlier period",
   184	                    utc(start), utc(end), if skipped == 1 { "" } else { "s" }, utc(old.unwrap_or(end)), schedule.target).into();
   185	            }
   186	        } else if held {
   187	            entry["holdCode"] = "disabled".into();
   188	            entry["reason"] = "schedules are held (dev --hold-schedules or --replay); a restart without the flag resumes at the next boundary".into();
   189	        } else if let Some(saved) = saved.filter(|saved| old.is_some() && (*saved > current || *saved == current && *saved > schedule.boundary(snapback4_core::now_ms()))) {
   190	            let due = schedule.next(saved);
   191	            entry["holdCode"] = "saved-boundary".into();
   192	            entry["savedBoundary"] = saved.into();
   193	            entry["heldUntil"] = utc(due).into();
   194	            entry["nextBoundary"] = utc(due).into();
   195	            entry["reason"] = format!("held by the store: boundary {} was already processed and this clock {} has not reached its next window (restarting resets the process offset, not the saved boundary). One fire per boundary. Advance {} more, or use a fresh staging deployment/store for an independent test; keep the same --clock-offset when continuing a test across serve restarts",
   196	                utc(saved), utc(now), span(due - now)).into();
   197	        } else if old != saved {
   198	            entry["holdCode"] = "initialized".into();
   199	            entry["reason"] = format!("{} started at boundary {}: a schedule fires at the boundary after the first one it sees, next {} ({} from now)",
   200	                if old.is_none() { "this schedule" } else { "this changed schedule" }, utc(saved.unwrap_or(current)), utc(next), span(next - now)).into();
   201	        } else {
   202	            entry["holdCode"] = "not-due".into();
   203	            entry["reason"] = format!("next boundary {} ({} from now; schedule times are UTC)", utc(next), span(next - now)).into();
   204	        }
   205	        entry
   206	    }).collect()
   207	}
   208	
   209	// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#10-wave-20-loop-repairs-dx5 — every declared schedule has one reported outcome.
   210	pub fn outcomes(schedules: &[serde_json::Value]) -> (Vec<serde_json::Value>, Vec<serde_json::Value>) {
   211	    schedules.iter().cloned().partition(|schedule| schedule["fired"] == true)
   212	}
   213	
   214	/// The report never loads full error/argument payloads from retained jobs.
   215	fn job_summaries(engine: &Engine) -> Result<std::collections::BTreeMap<String, serde_json::Value>, RuntimeError> {
   216	    let mut summaries = std::collections::BTreeMap::new();
   217	    for summary in engine.store().job_summaries(REPORT_ERROR_BYTES)? {
   218	        let name = summary.name;
   219	        if summaries.contains_key(&name) { continue; }
   220	        let mut error = summary.error;
   221	        let truncated = summary.error_truncated;
   222	        if let Some(text) = &mut error && truncated {
   223	            let mut end = REPORT_ERROR_BYTES.min(text.len());
   224	            while !text.is_char_boundary(end) { end -= 1; }
   225	            text.truncate(end); text.push('…');
   226	        }
   227	        summaries.insert(name, serde_json::json!({ "id":summary.id, "state":summary.state,
   228	            "rowsWritten":summary.rows_written, "error":error, "errorTruncated":truncated }));
   229	    }
   230	    Ok(summaries)
   231	}
   232	
   233	/// Startup lines for schedules the store holds: their saved boundary leads the clock.
   234	pub fn held_schedules(engine: &Engine, now: i64) -> Vec<String> {
   235	    engine.schema().schedules.iter().zip(saved_boundaries(engine)).filter_map(|(schedule, saved)| {
   236	        let saved = saved.filter(|saved| *saved > schedule.boundary(now))?;
   237	        let due = schedule.next(saved);
   238	        Some(format!("clock     schedule {} is held until {} ({} from now): its saved boundary {} is ahead of this clock; `clock advance {}` or restart with dev --resume-clock",
   239	            schedule.name, utc(due), span(due - now), utc(saved), format!("{}ms", due - now)))
   240	    }).collect()
   241	}

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	// Private coordination for app's prepare/commit/activate lifecycle. Keeping it
     2	// independent of either client tier avoids adding local code to online bundles.
     3	type Start = () => void | Promise<void>;
     4	const plans = new WeakMap<object, { start?: Start }>();
     5	
     6	export const startDeferred = (options: object): boolean => plans.has(options);
     7	
     8	export function deferStart(options: object, start: Start): boolean {
     9	  const plan = plans.get(options);
    10	  if (!plan) return false;
    11	  plan.start = start;
    12	  return true;
    13	}
    14	
    15	export async function prepareClient<T>(options: object, create: () => T | Promise<T>) {
    16	  const plan: { start?: Start } = {};
    17	  plans.set(options, plan);
    18	  try {
    19	    const client = await create();
    20	    return { client, start: async () => { await plan.start?.(); } };
    21	  } finally { plans.delete(options); }
    22	}

    25	// an already-running rotation, without starting a fresh rotation itself.
    26	const rotations = new WeakMap<object, () => Promise<unknown> | null>();
    27	export const trackRotation = (client: object, pending: () => Promise<unknown> | null): void => { rotations.set(client, pending); };
    28	export async function waitForRotation(client: object): Promise<void> {
    29	  try { await rotations.get(client)?.(); } catch { /* The client retains the outcome and any successor. */ }
    30	}

    14	// @ref LLP 3000.001#repair-b-never-install-a-confirmed-image-over-a-row-a-live-prediction-writes — open kept evidence before conditionally installing a backend.
    15	export function openLocalDevice(device: DeviceOpener, name: string, backend: BackendJson | null, startupRefusal?: BackendRefusalError) {
    16	  return device.open(name, null).catch(error => {
    17	    // Only never-synced permits first install; corruption/ownership cannot fall back.
    18	    const message = error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error);
    19	    if (!message.includes("E_AUTH") || !message.includes("this device has never synced: the server must be reachable once to open")) throw error;
    20	    if (!backend) throw startupRefusal ?? error;
    21	    return device.open(name, { initial_backend: backend });
    22	  });
    23	}

    25	export async function loadBackend(doFetch: typeof fetch, base: string, headers: Record<string, string>, signal?: AbortSignal): Promise<BackendJson> {
    26	  const { response, json } = await readWithAdmissionRetry(() => doFetch(`${base}/schema`, { headers, ...(signal ? { signal } : {}) }), () => !signal?.aborted, signal)
    27	    .catch(error => { throw new BackendRefusalError(transportRefusal(error)); });
    28	  const why = refusal(json.denied);
    29	  if (response.status === 401 && why?.code === "E_HTTP_RESPONSE") throw new BackendAuthError({ code: "E_AUTH", family: "auth", message: "the server refused this session" });
    30	  if (why?.code === "E_AUTH" && (response.status === 401 || !why.retryable)) throw new BackendAuthError(why);
    31	  if (why) throw new BackendRefusalError(why);
    32	  return json as unknown as BackendJson;
    33	}
    34	
    35	/** A first open retains a coded refusal for an offline kept-store fallback. */
    36	export async function initialBackend(fetchNow: typeof fetch, base: string, headers: Record<string, string>,
    37	  forget: () => Promise<unknown>, incompatible: (why: Refusal) => DeviceVersionError | undefined) {
    38	  try { return { backend: await loadBackend(fetchNow, base, headers) as BackendJson | null, refusal: undefined as BackendRefusalError | undefined, offline: false }; }
    39	  catch (error) {
    40	    if (error instanceof BackendRefusalError && incompatible(error.denied)) throw new DeviceVersionError(error.denied);
    41	    if (error instanceof BackendAuthError) { await forget(); throw new Error(`createLocalClient: ${error.denied.code}: ${error.denied.message}`); }
    42	    const refusal = error instanceof BackendRefusalError ? error : undefined;
    43	    return { backend: null, refusal, offline: !refusal || ["E_OFFLINE", "E_HTTP_RESPONSE"].includes(refusal.denied.code) };
    44	  }

codex
I found two possible material gaps to check against the frozen contract: App startup treats a staged session write as a completed save, and the held-boundary check appears to reject a normal advance within an already processed simulated window. I’m tracing both paths before finalizing the findings.
codex
One MATERIAL finding remains at `291b4ec653a5c2ada358eef8b1b4cd17df7e8bfd`:

**App startup bypasses the durable timing-save barrier.** The reconciliation in [local.ts:105](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b22-dx6/snapback4/packages/snapback4/src/local.ts:105) awaits `keepSession`, but during App preparation, [app.ts:442](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b22-dx6/snapback4/packages/snapback4/src/app.ts:442) acknowledges that write by updating only `scope.session`. The local constructor then persists the advanced device clock through `clock.attach`, before the actual credential commit at [app.ts:792](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b22-dx6/snapback4/packages/snapback4/src/app.ts:792).

If that final save remains held and the process exits, storage retains the old credential expiry alongside the advanced device clock. Offline reopening consequently rejects the still-live preserved session. The added failed-save Wasm test exercises direct `createLocalClient`, so it misses this `createApp({device})` startup path.

No other material findings remain. No builds or tests were run; ongoing qualification and author acceptance remain separate.
